# Bill's Books — Claude Code Session Guide

## What This App Does

Static GitHub Pages app that recommends books from Bill's personal to-read list and a curated external candidate pool. The recommendation engine scores candidates by cross-referencing his Goodreads history (ratings, themes, similar titles).

**Live site:** https://boilerbill83.github.io/my-books/

---

## Session Start Checklist

1. **Set the remote** (PAT is stored locally outside any git repo — never commit it or reference its path in committed files):
   ```bash
   PAT=$(cat ~/.github_pat)   # adjust to wherever your PAT lives locally
   git remote set-url origin https://${PAT}@github.com/boilerbill83/my-books.git
   ```
2. **Push to main** (deploy triggers on main only):
   ```bash
   git push origin HEAD:main
   ```

**Standing rule (Bill's explicit instruction): always push finished work to main — never stop to ask permission first.** Committing/pushing to main is the normal, expected end state of a working session here, not a risky action that needs sign-off each time.

**Standing rule (Bill's explicit instruction): always state times in EST when talking to Bill** — workflow run times, "started at," "check back in N minutes," etc. Convert from UTC (what GitHub Actions timestamps/logs report natively) before saying it out loud; don't just relay the raw UTC value. This is about spoken/written communication with Bill, not stored data — cron schedules in workflow YAML files stay in UTC (GitHub Actions requires it), and timestamps written into data files stay whatever format they already use.

---

## Project Structure

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `data/goodreadsData.json` | 939 books — all of Bill's Goodreads data |
| `data/candidatePool*.json` | External recommendation candidates (5 files, ~115 books) |
| `engine.js` | Scoring engine — buildIndexes, matchScore, confidenceScore, reason |
| `app.js` | UI + localStorage feedback persistence |
| `descSimilarity.js` | TF-IDF description signal (Session 12) |
| `data/enrichedMetadata.json` | Descriptions/categories/subjects cache (auto-filled daily) |
| `scripts/eval.js` | Honest precision@k eval — run before/after engine changes |
| `scripts/validate_review.js` | Rank tracker for the Session 12 review keeps — informational only since Session 34 (Bill's call), not a pass/fail gate; drift below top 25 reflects the pool's real, legitimate scoring improvements, not a regression by default |
| `audit_theme_tone.py` | Session 16c — researches each book's theme/tone accuracy via Claude + web search (real outside sources, not the cached description the classifier already used); logs verdicts to `output/tone-theme-audit-log.jsonl`; never writes to the data files directly |
| `scripts/apply_audit_corrections.mjs` | Session 16c — applies only high-confidence corrections from the audit log to `themes`/`tones` via the same surgical byte-span replace as the tone migration; marks them applied with a follow-up log line |
| `scripts/audit.js`, `scripts/full_audit.js` | Manual data-quality audits (duplicates, broken similarToTitles refs, missing themes) |
| `scripts/lib/loadData.js` | Shared loader — merges goodreadsData.json + candidate pools + enrichedMetadata.json + scrapedRatings.json + feedbackData.json into one row set. Used by both export_extract.js and data_quality_report.js so their join logic can't silently drift apart (see Session 13b: the Amazon-rating join bug). |
| `scripts/export_extract.js` | Weekly full extract — writes `output/all-books-YYYY-MM-DD.csv` |
| `scripts/data_quality_report.js` | Daily data quality report — per-field table (Field Name, Percent Populated, Quality Score, How Score is Determined, Suggestions for Improvement) via `FIELD_REGISTRY`; `critical: true` fields (feed a live scoring signal) get a stricter 90% threshold (🔴) vs 80% (⚠️) for everything else. Broken similarToTitles refs are checked against the engine's real title-matching normalization (paren-stripping) and split into near-miss (fixable typo/truncation) vs orphaned (no close match); non-canonical themes used ≥5 times are flagged as canonical-vocabulary-candidates, not just errors. An "Action Items" summary leads the report, plus a trend section (diffed against the most recent prior `output/data-quality-report-*.json` snapshot). Also computes a composite Data Quality Score (0-100, `computeQualityScore` — 5 weighted components: Critical Field Health, Field Completeness, Foundational Signal Integrity, Recommendation Safety, Data Hygiene; recalibrated twice in Session 14/14b) and a ranked Top-5-Impediments list, plus a "Roadmap to 100" (`buildRoadmap`, Session 14c) — a 6-phase cumulative simulation projecting the score after each phase of fixes by re-running the real scoring formula, not hand-typed estimates. Also computes a separate BBRE Accuracy Score (0-100, `computeBBREAccuracy`, Session 33) — not data completeness, but whether the engine's actual predictions are good, from `scripts/eval.js`'s shared `computeEvalMetrics()` (precision@10/25/50 + MAE-derived rating accuracy, weighted toward precision@10/25 per CLAUDE.md's own stated priority). Writes `output/data-quality-report-YYYY-MM-DD.md` + `.json` |
| `output/` | Dated CSV/report snapshots from the weekly jobs (one file per run, not overwritten), plus `data-quality-index.json` (manifest of snapshot dates for quality-dashboard.html) |
| `quality-dashboard.html` | Interactive dashboard reading the data-quality-report JSON snapshots — a "Last refreshed" timestamp at the top, a Data Quality Score ring gauge + component breakdown, a second BBRE Accuracy Score ring gauge (Session 33, engine-prediction quality via `scripts/eval.js`'s metrics, both dials share the generic `renderDial()`), At-a-Glance stat tiles, multi-week trend charts, sortable/searchable field, impact-score, and tag-count (themes/tones/subjects) tables, a tabbed integrity browser, and a Top Impediments section at the bottom. Linked from index.html's header. |
| `index.html` | Entry point |
| `trakt/index.html` / `trakt/discover.js` | Discover — "help me find something to watch" (Session 59 split, see below). Renders `trakt/data/dashboard.json` (stat tiles) plus, computed client-side via `trakt/engine.js`: a 🎯 Tonight's Top Pick hero, 🎲 Surprise Me (weighted-random draw from the real top 40), ⏱️ Got Two Hours?/📦 Weekend Binge time-budget shelves, 📼 Because You Loved… (grouped by which real 10/10 anchor drove the match), ▶️ Pick Up Where You Left Off (in-progress shows with a progress bar), the You'll Love panels, both Currently Airing sections, Genres You Rate Highest / Most-Watched Actors / You vs. The Crowd / Best Matches, a one-line taste blurb, and the "All Titles" sortable/searchable/CSV-exportable table. Linked from root `index.html`'s header as `./trakt/`. Loads `../styles.css` then `./dashboard.css`. Script tag is `type="module"` (imports from `engine.js` and `dashboardShared.js`). |
| `trakt/quality.html` / `trakt/quality.js` | Data Quality — "is the engine/data healthy" (Session 59 split). The 📈 Metadata & Engine Quality and 🎯 BMTRE Accuracy score dials, Predicted Score Distribution histograms, Biggest Prediction Misses (Best Matches' honest counterpart), Dismissal Reasons, Subjects, 📋 Field Population & Quality, and 🛠️ Improvement Opportunities (all currently-open findings from the full `trakt/` code review). Loads `../styles.css` then `./dashboard.css`. Script tag is `type="module"` (imports from `engine.js` and `dashboardShared.js`). |
| `trakt/dashboardShared.js` | Page-agnostic helpers both `discover.js` and `quality.js` import — formatting (`esc`/`fmtNum`/`fmtCompact`), poster/title/status-tag rendering, the bar-chart/dial SVG primitives, the genre/subgenre/subject/era taxonomy display labels, CSV export, `initCollapsibleCards()`, and two new shared functions: `loadAllData()` (the one fetch-all-10-files-plus-OMDb-merge loader both pages call, so the file list can't drift between them) and `predictedVsActualRows()` (the shared row-builder behind both Best Matches and Prediction Misses, so the two pages can never disagree about a title's predicted score). Same discipline as `scripts/lib/loadData.js` on the book side and `trakt/scripts/lib/traktExport.js` on this side. |
| `trakt/dashboard.css` | Shared CSS for Discover and Quality (the classes both pages actually share — cards, tables, dials, meters, rec-card styles) — extracted from the original single-page dashboard's inline `<style>` block rather than duplicated across two files. Each page still keeps its own page-specific rules inline (Discover: hero/Surprise-Me/Because-You-Loved/shelf/progress-bar styles new to the split). |
| `trakt/data/dashboard.json` | Committed, compact (~16KB) summary built by `scripts/build_trakt_dashboard.js` from Bill's full Trakt data-export zip. The only Trakt-derived file this repo commits from the raw export — the ~5.6MB/89-file export itself is never committed. Refreshed by re-running the script against a new export and pushing; see "BMTRE" below. **This project never calls the Trakt API** — the export is a manual download-and-upload Bill does himself. |
| `trakt/data/library.json` / `watchlist.json` / `currentlyWatching.json` | BMTRE's own data (Session 44) — built by `trakt/scripts/build_trakt_library.js` from the same export, independent of `dashboard.json`. `library.json` is every watched movie/show with `myRating` (Trakt's native 1-10 scale) — the `goodreadsData.json` analog. `watchlist.json` is the unwatched, explicitly-queued titles — the `to-read` analog. `currentlyWatching.json` is a derived view (shows with `completionStatus: 'in-progress'`). Upserted, not overwritten, on re-run — a title missing from a fresh export is kept and flagged, never silently deleted. |
| `trakt/data/candidatePool.json` | Discovered-but-unwatched titles (Session 46) — BMTRE's candidate pool *beyond* the watchlist, built by `trakt/scripts/discover_candidates.js` from the `similarToIds`/`recommendedIds` TMDB already returned for loved (9-10 rated) enriched library titles. Bare-id stubs (`title`/`year` null) until `enrich_tmdb.py` backfills them from TMDB's own response. Pure local computation — no network calls, since the citation network already lives in `enrichedMetadata.json`. Ranked and capped **per type** (movie/show separately), not combined — Bill has ~2x as many loved shows as loved movies, so a single combined ranking structurally starves movies of a chance to even reach the pool (found and fixed this same session the movie score-ceiling investigation traced to it). Capped at 100 movies / 100 shows by `trakt/scripts/prune_candidate_pool.js` (Bill's explicit request) — see that script's row below. |
| `trakt/data/discoveredHistory.json` | Append-only record of every titleKey `discover_candidates.js` has ever added to `candidatePool.json`, whether still present or since evicted by pruning. Needed once discovery + pruning both run on a recurring schedule (the weekly job below): without it, a candidate evicted one week for scoring low would look brand-new again the next week (pruning deletes it from the only place the old "known" check looked), get rediscovered, and likely get re-evicted in a permanent loop. A title is only ever evaluated once. |
| `trakt/data/enrichedMetadata.json` | TMDB content-metadata cache (genres, overview, cast/crew, keywords, community rating, TMDB's own similar/recommendations id lists), keyed by `titleKey`. Filled by the daily `trakt-enrich-tmdb.yml` workflow via `trakt/enrich_tmdb.py` — the `enrichedMetadata.json` analog. Starts empty (`{}`); BMTRE degrades gracefully (an honest "not enough data yet" reason) for any title not yet enriched. |
| `trakt/data/feedbackData.json` | Dismissals/corrections (Session 44, starts empty) — the `feedbackData.json` analog. Not yet wired to any UI in Phase 1; exists so `trakt/engine.js`'s exclusion logic has somewhere real to read from once a dismiss feature is built. |
| `trakt/engine.js` / `trakt/recommend.html` / `trakt/recommend.js` | BMTRE (Bill's Movies & TV Recommendation Engine, Session 44) — Phase 1 rule-based scorer + minimal ranked-list UI. See "BMTRE" below for the full design. Linked from `trakt/index.html`'s header as `./recommend.html`. |
| `trakt/ENGINE.md` | Full reference doc for how BMTRE scores a candidate — every signal in `baseSignals()`/`omdbSignal()`, its exact weight/cap, and why (measured distribution or `eval.js`-validated), plus the hard candidate filters, the subgenre/tone taxonomy, the eval harness, and a quick-reference weight table. Written directly from a full read of `engine.js` (not from the session log), so it's the accurate source for "what does signal X actually weigh" — CLAUDE.md's own BMTRE section below stays the session-history/design-rationale record, this file is the current-state mechanics reference. **Standing rule (Bill's explicit instruction): update this file in the same commit as any `trakt/engine.js` change that adds, removes, or re-weights a scoring signal or filter** — a new signal, a changed constant, a new hard filter, a signal moving from display-only to scored (or vice versa). Not required for a pure refactor, comment-only change, or a change to a display-only function §4 already documents as unscored. |
| `trakt/scripts/lib/traktExport.js` | Shared raw-export reader — both `scripts/build_trakt_dashboard.js` and `trakt/scripts/build_trakt_library.js` import this rather than each re-implementing their own globbing/parsing, the same discipline `scripts/lib/loadData.js` established for the book project's join logic. |
| `trakt/scripts/prune_candidate_pool.js` | Caps `candidatePool.json` at a fixed size per type (default 100 movies + 100 shows, Bill's explicit request). Scores every candidate with the *exact same* `matchScore()`/`buildIndexes()` `trakt/engine.js`'s `rankAll()` uses to render "New pick" cards — no separate ranking logic to drift out of sync — keeps the top N per type, evicts the rest. Never evicts a candidate with no `enrichedMetadata.json` entry yet regardless of the cap (a freshly-discovered stub would score near-zero on missing data alone; it competes fairly only once enriched). Also sweeps out anything gone stale (watched/watchlisted since being added) as a bonus hygiene pass. **Fixed a real data-loss bug**: a dismissed/feedback-excluded candidate used to be folded into the same "stale, delete outright" bucket as already-watched/watchlisted titles — meaning every dismissal got permanently erased from `candidatePool.json` on the very next prune run, with the loss invisible to `export_extract.js`'s full data extract (which only reads library/watchlist/candidatePool.json, so a deleted-and-dismissed title vanished from the daily export too, even though `feedbackData.json` still remembered the dismissal). Found via a real audit — all 18 of Bill's actual dismissed titles were missing from the export; their TMDB metadata was still intact in `enrichedMetadata.json`, so all 18 were restored to `candidatePool.json`. Excluded candidates are now kept unconditionally (never deleted), just never scored or competing for a cap slot — the same disposition an unenriched stub already gets. Run manually or as the last step of `trakt-discover-candidates.yml` below. |
| `trakt/scripts/lib/loadAllTitles.js` / `trakt/scripts/export_extract.js` | Full daily data extract (Bill's explicit request: "a new output file every day with all metadata and all rows including candidates"). `loadAllTitles.js` is the shared full-row loader — same role `scripts/lib/loadData.js` plays for the book project — merging `library.json`/`watchlist.json`/`candidatePool.json` into one row set tagged by status (Watched/Watchlist/Candidate), joined against TMDB metadata, OMDb metadata, feedback/exclusion state, and the engine's own live-computed scores (56 columns, not a curated UI subset). `export_extract.js` writes `trakt/output/all-titles-YYYY-MM-DD.csv` via the daily `trakt-export-extract.yml` workflow below. |
| `trakt/enrich_tmdb.py` / `.github/workflows/trakt-enrich-tmdb.yml` | Daily TMDB enrichment, mirroring `enrich_metadata.py`/`enrich-metadata.yml`'s resumable-via-cache-membership pattern exactly. Needs a `TMDB_API_KEY` repo secret. `REFRESH_ALL=1` (or `--refresh-all`, or the workflow's `refresh_all` input) re-fetches every title regardless of cache membership — a one-off backfill for when `extract_entry()` gains a field (e.g. `originalLanguage`, `imdbId`) that already-cached entries don't carry, since the cache only stores the extracted subset, not the raw TMDB response. `get_json()` captures and surfaces TMDB's own error-response body on a non-2xx status (not just the bare code) — added after a real dead-key incident where a bare 401 gave no way to tell "revoked" from "malformed" from "suspended for volume" without this. |
| `.github/workflows/trakt-discover-candidates.yml` | Weekly (Sundays 10 AM UTC, after both daily enrichment jobs), plus `workflow_dispatch` with tunable inputs. Runs `discover_candidates.js` → `enrich_tmdb.py` → `prune_candidate_pool.js` in sequence so the candidate pool keeps refreshing itself between sessions instead of going stale. The prune step has `if: always()` so a bad/expired TMDB key doesn't also block pruning the already-enriched backlog — it just leaves that run's new discoveries unscored until a future run can enrich them. |
| `.github/workflows/trakt-export-extract.yml` | Daily 12:00 UTC (after both TMDB/OMDb enrichment jobs and the Sunday discovery job), plus `workflow_dispatch`. Runs `trakt/scripts/export_extract.js`, commits the dated `trakt/output/all-titles-YYYY-MM-DD.csv` (previous dates kept, never overwritten — same convention as the book side's `export-extract.yml`). Same commit-with-retry pattern as the other Trakt workflows. |
| `trakt/output/` | Dated CSV snapshots from `trakt-export-extract.yml` (one file per day, not overwritten) — the `output/` analog for the Trakt side. |
| `trakt/enrich_omdb.py` / `.github/workflows/trakt-enrich-omdb.yml` | Daily OMDb enrichment (Session 49) — Rotten Tomatoes/Metacritic scores and Oscar/Emmy awards data, neither of which TMDB has. Keyed by IMDb id (library/watchlist titles carry `ids.imdb` natively from the Trakt export; candidatePool stubs get theirs via `enrich_tmdb.py`'s `external_ids` append, cached as `imdbId`). Cached separately in `trakt/data/omdbMetadata.json` (starts `{}`) rather than merged into `enrichedMetadata.json`, so the source of every signal stays traceable — same discipline the book side keeps between Google Books/Open Library and the Amazon scrape. Needs an `OMDB_API_KEY` repo secret (free tier, 1,000 req/day at omdbapi.com/apikey.aspx) — created and working as of the same session that added the daily extract; `trakt/engine.js`'s `audienceScore()`/`awardsScore()` are now wired into `matchScore()` via `omdbSignal()` (weights measured against the real dataset — see the session log for the exact calibration) rather than display-only. |

## Perfect Book Entry — Quality Standard

A **perfect** book entry in `goodreadsData.json` has all of the following:

### Required fields (every book)
```json
{
  "title": "Exact title as shown on Goodreads",
  "author": "Author Name",
  "shelf": "read | to-read | currently-reading",
  "myRating": 0,
  "pages": 320,
  "avgRating": 4.12,
  "ratingsCount": 85000,
  "year": 2021
}
```

### Quality fields (must be present and correct)
```json
{
  "themes": ["thriller", "psychological", "domestic suspense"],
  "similarToTitles": ["The Silent Patient", "Behind Closed Doors", "Verity"]
}
```

### Quality rules

| Field | Rule |
|-------|------|
| `themes` | 2–5 tags; must use **canonical vocabulary only** (see below); must match the book's actual genre |
| `similarToTitles` | 3–5 entries; every title must be an **exact character-for-character match** to another book's `title` field in the dataset — the engine uses `Set.has()` for matching, so "The Firm" ≠ "The Firm (The Firm, #1)" |
| `pages` | Non-zero integer; 0 or missing means the pages-fit bonus never fires |
| `ratingsCount` | Positive integer; missing means the popularity bonus never fires |
| `myRating` | 0 (unrated) to 5; 0-star read books don't contribute to `authorRatingWeight` |
| `avgRating` | Goodreads community average; affects `(avg - 3.5) * 10` score term |

### Canonical theme vocabulary

Use **only** these values. Do not invent new tags without updating the engine's fallback constants.

**Fiction / Genre:**
`thriller`, `psychological`, `suspense`, `domestic suspense`, `mystery`, `crime`, `noir`, `horror`, `high-concept`, `spy`, `adventure`, `historical`, `YA`, `romance`, `contemporary`, `speculative`, `sci-fi`, `social commentary`, `legal`, `courtroom`

**Nonfiction:**
`memoir`, `biography`, `true crime`, `history`, `tech history`, `finance`, `business`, `sports`, `food`, `music history`, `political`, `military`, `psychology`

**Unassigned (use sparingly):**
`humor`, `comedy`

### How themes drive scoring

The engine builds a `fiveStarThemes` map from all 5-star read books. Themes are scored per-book with these thresholds:

| Count in 5★ reads | Bonus per matching theme |
|---|---|
| ≥ 40 | +5 |
| ≥ 25 | +4 |
| ≥ 12 | +3 |
| ≥ 4  | +2 |
| ≥ 1  | +1 |

Cap: +8 total per book regardless of theme count.

Current top themes (as of Session 21, Jul 2026 — `narrative nonfiction` and `literary` both retired, see session log): `thriller` (105), `memoir` (39), `contemporary` (36), `psychological` (33), `speculative` (33), `suspense` (31), `sports` (27), `history` (27), `legal` (25), `crime` (23).

### How similarToTitles drives scoring

Two separate engine signals:

1. **Forward match** (candidate → 5★ read): If a to-read/candidate book's `similarToTitles` contains a title that is in the user's 5★ reads → **+8 pts per match**.

2. **Reverse index** (5★ read → candidate): If a 5★ read's `similarToTitles` contains a to-read book's title → **+6 pts per citing 5★ read, capped at +12**. This fires on the `reverseSimilar` map built in `buildIndexes`.

**Critical:** Both signals require exact title string matches. Always verify against the actual title stored in the JSON. Common pitfalls:
- Series notation: "The Firm" vs "The Firm (The Firm, #1)" — use the full title
- Subtitle truncation: "Bourdain: The Definitive Oral Biography" not "Bourdain"
- Subtitle differences: "Quiet: The Power of Introverts..." vs "Quiet Power: The Secret Strengths..."

### Canonical Tone Vocabulary (redesigned Session 16)

Use **only** these 24 values in a book's `tones` field. Unlike themes (which describe genre/subject — *what a book is about*), tones describe *how a book reads* — pacing, mood, voice, craft — so the same word can and should apply across fiction and nonfiction alike: a legal thriller and a memoir can both be `tense`; a domestic-suspense novel and a Silicon Valley exposé can both be `twisty`.

**Pacing & structure:** `propulsive`, `compulsive`, `slow-burn`, `twisty`, `procedural`, `nonlinear`, `ensemble`
**Mood & emotional register:** `dark`, `bleak`, `tense`, `heartwarming`, `poignant`, `inspiring`
**Voice & humor:** `funny`, `satirical`, `conversational`
**Craft & style:** `atmospheric`, `lyrical`, `gritty`, `character-driven`
**Intellectual register:** `revelatory`, `dense`, `thoughtful`, `investigative`

A few of these sound genre-adjacent but aren't — `procedural` describes information unfolding step-by-step (a legal procedural, a business turnaround, a true-crime investigation all qualify) and `investigative` describes an exposé/dogged-reporting *voice* (fits business, tech, and political nonfiction alike), not a specific subject. Verified zero string overlap with the 37 canonical themes, and zero books carry the same value in both `themes` and `tones`. Engine code never conflates the two fields either: `themeBonus()`/`primaryTheme()` read only `.themes`; `toneSignal()`/`buildToneProfile()`/`primaryTone()`/`inferTones()` read only `.tones`.

**Balance rule:** no tone may sit on more than 15% of the dataset (~166 of 1,111 books as of Session 16). This isn't cosmetic — `bbreEngine.js`'s `buildToneProfile()`/`toneSignal()` computes a real per-tone rating-preference delta from Bill's history and applies it live to every candidate. A tone used on a third of the dataset (as `accessible`/`narrative-driven`/`fast-paced` were under the old free-form vocabulary) can't produce a meaningful signal — it's just "most books." 2–4 tones per book, same as before.

Enforced in three places, which must be kept in sync if the vocabulary ever changes: `bbreEngine.js`'s `THEME_TONES_MAP` (theme→tone fallback for a book with no real tags) and `TONE_PRIORITY` (tie-break order), `tag_with_haiku.py`'s `TONES` constant, and `app.js`'s `TONE_FILTER_ORDER` (the live site's tone filter chips). `scripts/data_quality_report.js`'s `findNonCanonicalTones()` flags any future drift back toward free-form tags (mirrors the theme vocabulary's equivalent check).

## Adding a New Book

When adding a new book to `goodreadsData.json`, a complete entry looks like:

```json
{
  "title": "The Last Trial",
  "author": "Scott Turow",
  "shelf": "to-read",
  "myRating": 0,
  "pages": 384,
  "avgRating": 4.01,
  "ratingsCount": 12500,
  "year": 2020,
  "themes": ["legal", "thriller", "courtroom"],
  "similarToTitles": ["Presumed Innocent", "A Time to Kill", "The Client"]
}
```

- `similarToTitles` values must be exact matches to `title` fields in the dataset
- Check the canonical theme list above before inventing a new tag

---

## Engine Summary (key scoring terms for to-read books)

| Signal | Max pts | Source |
|--------|---------|--------|
| fromToRead base | +10 | shelf === "to-read" |
| 5★ author bonus | +15 | `fiveStarAuthors` map |
| Author rating weight | +7.5 | `authorRatingWeight` (weighted by rating: 5★=+1.0, 4★=+0.8, 3★=+0.3, 2★=−0.5, 1★=−1.0) |
| Similar-author bonus | +15/author | `candidate.similarToAuthors` ∩ fiveStarAuthors/authorRatingWeight (same formula as pool books) |
| Forward title match | +8/match | candidate.similarToTitles ∩ fiveStarTitles |
| Reverse title match | +12 | `reverseSimilar` (5★ reads citing this book) |
| Theme bonus | +8 | `themeBonus()` vs fiveStarThemes |
| Popularity bonus | +4 | `ratingsCountBonus()` (>100k ratings) |
| Community rating | variable | `(avgRating - 3.5) * 10` |
| Pages fit | ±4–6 | vs medianPages of read books |

---

## Evaluation Discipline (added Session 12 — do not skip)

Run `node scripts/eval.js` BEFORE and AFTER any engine change. It reports
precision@k over completed rated reads (leave-one-out, DNFs excluded — DNF
virtual ratings leak the answer and inflate metrics; header Spearman claims
predate this fix). Baseline as of Jul 3 2026: p10=100, p25=96, p50=96,
MAE=0.770. Top-of-list precision (p10/p25) outranks MAE: never trade it away.

**Measured dead ends — do not re-attempt without new data:**
- Author/theme weight tuning (upweighting low ratings, asymmetric variance
  penalty, halving PRIOR_K): flat or traded p25 for MAE. The model is at its
  ceiling; completed 2★ books look identical to 5★ books in all features.
- Negative-only description voting: strictly worse than symmetric.

**Real bottleneck:** negative training data. Only 39 dismissals and 106
low-rated completions whose features match loved books.

## TF-IDF Description Signal (descSimilarity.js, Session 12)

k-NN over TF-IDF vectors of real descriptions from data/enrichedMetadata.json
(filled daily by the enrich-metadata workflow, 150 books/run). Coverage-gated:
inactive until 150+ rated reads have descriptions. Tunables in exported CFG
(k=12, cap=6 — sweep-confirmed optimal). Flows: app.js → rankBBRE(…, meta) →
buildTasteModel(…, meta) → Signal 4b in predictRating.

## Trakt Dashboard — Session 41-43

A read-only dashboard living alongside the book app in its own `trakt/` folder (unrelated data, same static-site conventions), visualizing Bill's real Trakt watch history, ratings, watchlist, and favorites — built from a data export Bill downloads from his own Trakt account and uploads here.

**The Trakt API is never called by this project — a standing rule, not a preference.** Two earlier approaches were tried and rejected: Session 41's OAuth device-code live-sync (Trakt's live-import path needs VIP, Bill doesn't want any API dependency, and the sandbox's network policy blocks `api.trakt.tv` outright anyway), then Session 41-42's manual browser-based tracker + hand-built CSV import file. Both are now retired. **The current architecture only ever reads a file Bill exports and uploads himself** — Trakt's account-level "Data Export" feature (Settings → Data on trakt.tv; a zip of ~89 JSON files: watched history, ratings, watchlist, favorites, stats, profile — NOT the same thing as the CSV *import* format documented in Session 42's history below). Do not add a script or UI feature that calls `api.trakt.tv` without Bill explicitly asking for it.

**Folder layout:**
```
trakt/
  index.html          — the dashboard UI
  dashboard.js         — fetches and renders trakt/data/dashboard.json
  data/
    dashboard.json      — committed, compact (~16KB) summary; the only Trakt-derived
                           file this repo commits (the raw export is not committed)
```
`trakt/index.html` links back to the book app via `../index.html` and pulls the shared stylesheet via `../styles.css`. Root `index.html`'s header links to `./trakt/`.

**Importing a fresh export — see "Importing a fresh Trakt export" below (the authoritative, full step-by-step; this subsection used to duplicate a shorter version of it and drifted out of sync with the BMTRE section's own copy, so both were consolidated into one place after Session 48).**

**Known real data caveat, handled explicitly rather than hidden:** a large share of episode watch events (69.1% / 7,523 of 10,894 as of the Session 43 export) carry a `1970-01-01` epoch placeholder instead of a real `watched_at` timestamp — a bulk Plex-sync import with no per-episode date, not a bug in this pipeline. `build_trakt_dashboard.js` excludes these from the year-over-year activity chart (rather than plotting a false 1970 spike) and reports the exact undated share in `dataCaveats`, which the dashboard renders as a visible callout under the episodes-by-year chart. Movie watch events are all cleanly dated (they came from Session 42's real CSV import, built from verified release dates) — this caveat is episode-only.

**History (Session 41-42, kept for context — fully retired, not the current design):** the original version was a manual add-a-movie browser tool (`localStorage` → "Copy JSON" → commit, same pattern as `app.js`'s feedback persistence) paired with a hand-built Trakt *import* CSV, resolved via background-Agent web research since IMDb ids/release dates aren't something Bill provides directly. That CSV format took two rejected guesses to get right against Trakt's real importer — the working schema (`imdb_id,type,watched_at,watchlisted_at,rating,rated_at`, bare ids under a per-service column name, full ISO 8601 datetimes, `watchlisted_at` present even when empty) is preserved here only because a *different* future import need might resurface it, not because the dashboard uses it:
```
imdb_id        — bare IMDb id, NO prefix, e.g. tt1160419 (the column name itself
                 identifies the service — trakt_id/tmdb_id/tvdb_id are the alternatives)
type           — movie | episode | show | season
watched_at     — full ISO 8601 datetime, e.g. 2019-12-25T00:00:00Z (a bare date is rejected)
watchlisted_at — full ISO 8601 datetime; always include the column even if empty
rating         — 1-10 integer, optional
rated_at       — full ISO 8601 datetime; only parsed if rating is present
```
Once Bill actually uploaded his real Trakt export (Session 43), it was confirmed to exactly match the 124 hand-built movies, making the manual tool fully redundant — it and its data files (`trakt.js`, `data/watched.json`, `data/watchlist.json`) were deleted rather than kept alongside the dashboard.

## BMTRE — Movies & TV Recommendation Engine (Session 44)

A real recommendation engine for movies/TV, modeled closely on BBRE (the book engine above) — same signal shapes, same "explain the score" discipline, ported to a domain where every title carries an exact TMDB id instead of a fuzzy title/author match. Built by first having a research agent extract BBRE's exact mechanics (`engine.js`/`bbreEngine.js`/`rateEngine.js`/`descSimilarity.js`/`enrich_metadata.py`) as ground truth, then a design agent port each mechanic against that reference — full plan preserved at the time of writing in this session's plan-mode history. **Phase 1 only** (this session) — a working end-to-end pipeline: ingest → enrich → score → recommend, using the real 28-item watchlist as the only candidate pool. No Bayesian predictor, no candidate discovery beyond the watchlist, no tone tagging, no dismissal/diversity layer yet — all explicitly deferred to future sessions, the same incremental path BBRE itself took across 40+ sessions rather than one big-bang build.

**Two facts anchor the whole design:**
- Every title already carries `ids.tmdb` (from the Trakt export) — so `titleKey = \`${type}:${tmdbId}\`` is the join key everywhere, never `norm(title)`-style fuzzy matching. This sidesteps the entire bug class BBRE's own history had to fix repeatedly (e.g. Session 13b's Amazon-rating join, series-notation title mismatches).
- TMDB is unreachable from an interactive session (`api.themoviedb.org` → `HTTP 000` tested directly, `omdbapi.com` → `403`) — same as Google Books/Open Library for BBRE. All enrichment runs via `.github/workflows/trakt-enrich-tmdb.yml`, never called live.

### Metadata schema (Phase 1 fields)

**Identity** (from the Trakt export, via `trakt/scripts/build_trakt_library.js`): `type` (`movie`\|`show`), `titleKey`, `ids.{trakt,imdb,tmdb,slug,tvdb}`, `title`, `year`.

**Personal activity** (same source): `myRating` (**1-10, Trakt's native scale — do not reuse BBRE's 1-5 rating-weight curve unmodified**), `ratedAt`, `plays`, `lastWatchedAt` (caution: can itself carry the `1970-01-01` epoch placeholder the dashboard's `dataCaveats` already documents — don't trust it for recency signals), `airedEpisodes` (shows), `completionStatus` (derived: `caught-up`\|`in-progress`\|`unknown` — **informational only in Phase 1, not consumed by scoring**; Trakt's export has no explicit "I dropped this show" signal, so a dropped-show penalty needs real feedback data first, same caution BBRE's own DNF-penalty history teaches), `favorite`, `watchlisted`/`watchlistedAt`.

**TMDB-enriched** (via `trakt/enrich_tmdb.py`, keyed by `titleKey` in `enrichedMetadata.json`): `genres` (TMDB's own fixed ~19/16-genre vocabulary — no invented-tag drift risk, unlike BBRE's free-form theme history), `overview`, `keywords` (free-form, secondary signal, not yet consumed by scoring), `runtime`/`episodeRunTime`, `releaseDate`/`firstAirDate`, `director` (movie, from crew credits where `job === 'Director'`) / `createdBy` (show, native field), `topCast` (cached but not yet scored — deferred, see port table below), `belongsToCollection` (cached but not yet scored — franchise signal, deferred), `voteAverage`/`voteCount`/`popularity`, `status`/`numberOfSeasons`/`numberOfEpisodes` (shows), **`similarToIds` + `recommendedIds`** (from TMDB's own `/similar` + `/recommendations` endpoints — the single biggest structural advantage over BBRE: no manual/LLM curation needed to build a similar-title network at all).

### Engine port — BBRE mechanism → BMTRE Phase 1

| BBRE (`engine.js`) | BMTRE (`trakt/engine.js`) | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `norm()`/`bookKey(title,author)` | `titleKey(type, tmdbId)` = `` `${type}:${tmdbId}` `` | Exact, no fuzzy matching needed at all |
| `buildIndexes()` | same shape: `watched`, `lovedTitles`, `lovedCreators`/`creatorRatingWeight`, `lovedGenres`, `reverseSimilar`, `excluded` | "Loved" = `myRating >= 9` (top ~26% of Bill's real distribution: 130 of 495 ratings are 9-10) |
| `fiveStarAuthors`/`authorRatingWeight` | `lovedCreators`/`creatorRatingWeight`, keyed on **director (movies) or primary creator (shows)** via `getCreator()` | Cast (top-billed actors) is cached in `enrichedMetadata.json` but **not yet a scoring signal** — structurally closer to BBRE's `similarToAuthors` bridging role, needs more design thought before porting |
| Rating-weight curve (1-5 scale, `r>=5→1.0…r<2→-1.0`) | `ratingWeight(r) = clamp((r - 6.5) / 3.5, -1, 1)` | Recalibrated against Bill's real 1-10 distribution (mode/median ~7-8, not the scale midpoint) — not a linear rescale of BBRE's curve |
| `themeBonus()` | `genreBonus()`, same tier-by-loved-count shape | Tier thresholds (`≥60/35/18/6/1`) are **provisional placeholders** — can't be calibrated against a real histogram until `enrichedMetadata.json` has real genre data from a live enrichment run |
| Forward `similarToTitles` match (+8/match) | `similarToIds`/`recommendedIds` id-exact match, capped `min(24, matches*8)` | Not gated to watchlist-only the way BBRE's forward match effectively is — TMDB coverage is comprehensive, not sparse hand-curated data |
| Reverse `reverseSimilar` match (to-read only, capped +12) | Same formula, **not gated to watchlist-only** — applies to any candidate | Same reasoning as above |
| `(avgRating-3.5)*10` | `(voteAverage - 6.0) * 8` | TMDB-only in Phase 1, no multi-source bias-offset yet (BBRE only added Amazon after Goodreads-only had matured — same sequencing here) |
| `ratingsCountBonus()` | `voteCountBonus()`, tiers retuned to TMDB's vote_count ranges | |
| pages-fit vs `medianPages` | **not ported** | Runtime isn't an analogous completion-risk signal; skipped entirely rather than forcing a bad analogy |
| `recencyBonus()` | Same shape, movie/show curves not yet split independently | |
| repeat-exposure penalty (`timesShown`) | **not ported yet** | No persistent "times shown" tracking exists without a real feedback UI loop — deferred with `dismissAdjust`/MMR/diversity below |
| `confidenceScore()`, `reason()` | Ported directly — same "explain the score" pattern, same tiebreaker role (never affects rank on its own) | |

**Explicitly deferred to Phase 2+** (needs either more rated volume or real feedback data to calibrate against, same discipline BBRE always used via `scripts/eval.js` rather than guessing): a Bayesian predictor (`rateEngine.js` port), the combine/adjustment/MMR-diversity layer (`bbreEngine.js` port), candidate discovery beyond the watchlist (`discover_candidates.py`, seeded from loved titles' TMDB `/similar`+`/recommendations`), tone tagging (`tag_with_haiku.py` port), `descSimilarity.js` port, franchise/rewatch/dropped-show signals, and a data-quality report for this dataset.

### Importing a fresh Trakt export (Session 48) — the authoritative workflow

The one and only place this is documented — the Trakt Dashboard section above and this section used to each carry their own shorter, drifting copy; Session 48 merged them here so there's a single source of truth, the same discipline `scripts/lib/loadData.js` established for the book project's join logic. Whenever Bill uploads a fresh Trakt export zip (Settings → Data on trakt.tv), do all of these steps, in order — skipping the later ones (especially re-enrichment) is what "properly import" means, not just refreshing the dashboard:

1. **Extract and verify.** `unzip -q -o <zip> -d <scratch-dir>`, then confirm the extracted file count matches `unzip -l <zip>`'s own reported total (89 files as of Session 48's export shape — watched history, ratings, watchlist, favorites, stats, profile). A truncated extraction happened once in Session 43 from a harness interruption mid-command, not a corrupt zip — don't skip this check.
2. **Rebuild the dashboard summary.** `node scripts/build_trakt_dashboard.js <scratch-dir>` — reads `user-stats.json`, `watched-movies.json`, `watched-shows*.json`, `watched-history-*.json`, `ratings-movies.json`, `ratings-shows*.json`, `lists-watchlist.json`, `lists-favorites.json`; writes `trakt/data/dashboard.json` (~16KB, the only Trakt-derived file besides the ones below that this repo commits — the raw export itself never is).
3. **Rebuild the recommendation-engine data.** `node trakt/scripts/build_trakt_library.js <scratch-dir>` — upserts `trakt/data/library.json` (every watched movie/show + `myRating`), `trakt/data/watchlist.json` (unwatched, explicitly-queued titles), and `trakt/data/currentlyWatching.json` (derived, in-progress shows). Upsert, not overwrite — a title missing from the fresh export is kept and flagged, never silently deleted, since a partial/older export shouldn't erase real history.
4. **Prune the candidate pool of anything Bill has since watched or watchlisted for real.** `trakt/data/candidatePool.json` (built by `discover_candidates.js`/`resolve_titles.py`) only excludes already-known titles *at the moment a candidate is added* — if Bill later watches or Trakt-watchlists one of those candidates directly (outside this pipeline, as happened for real in Session 48 with "Tom Clancy's Jack Ryan"), the stub goes stale and should be removed so it doesn't linger as dead data:
   ```js
   const libKeys = new Set(library.titles.map(t => t.titleKey));
   const wlKeys = new Set(watchlist.titles.map(t => t.titleKey));
   pool.titles = pool.titles.filter(t => !libKeys.has(t.titleKey) && !wlKeys.has(t.titleKey));
   ```
   Note this is a hygiene step, not a correctness requirement as of Session 48 — `trakt/engine.js`'s `rankAll()` already defensively excludes any candidate whose titleKey is in `idx.watched` or the real watchlist before it ever reaches the dashboard's "New pick" cards, so a stale stub can't actually surface as a bad recommendation even if this step is skipped. Do it anyway; a growing pile of dead stubs is still worth avoiding.
5. **Commit + push every changed file together** (`dashboard.json`, `library.json`, `watchlist.json`, `currentlyWatching.json` if it changed, `candidatePool.json` if step 4 pruned anything) — one commit for the routine data refresh is fine; a separate commit only if step 4's pruning is bundled with an unrelated code fix (as it was in Session 48, alongside the `rankAll()` hardening above).
6. **Enrich the new titles — don't just wait for the daily cron.** Any title in the fresh `library.json`/`watchlist.json`/`candidatePool.json` without a matching key in `enrichedMetadata.json` is *eventually* picked up automatically by the next scheduled run of `trakt-enrich-tmdb.yml` (same resumable-via-cache-membership idiom as `enrich_metadata.py` — the cache itself is the resume cursor), but the daily schedule only processes 150 titles and reads `watchlist.json` → `library.json` → `candidatePool.json` in strict priority order (Session 46 hit this directly: a default-sized batch got fully consumed by library backlog before ever reaching new candidates). After a real import, check how many titles are actually pending before deciding whether to wait or trigger manually:
   ```js
   const cache = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('trakt/data/enrichedMetadata.json'));
   const pending = list => list.titles.filter(t => t.titleKey && !cache[t.titleKey]).length;
   ```
   If it's more than a handful, trigger `trakt-enrich-tmdb.yml` manually via `workflow_dispatch` with a `batch_size` input comfortably above the pending count (e.g. Session 48's 60 pending titles → `batch_size: 100`) rather than waiting on the next 8 AM UTC run.
7. **Pull the enrichment commit back into the working branch** once the workflow finishes (it commits straight to `main`) before doing any further work against `enrichedMetadata.json` locally.
8. **Nothing else is manual.** `trakt/index.html`/`recommend.html` just re-read whatever's committed — no code changes are needed for a routine import, only for the enrichment/pruning steps above.

**Requires a `TMDB_API_KEY` repo secret** — Bill creates a free API key at themoviedb.org (instant approval, personal use), same pattern as `GOOGLE_BOOKS_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Without it, `trakt/recommend.html` and the dashboard's recommendation panels still run correctly but every title shows an honest "not enough data yet" reason (verified in Session 44 with all 28 real watchlist titles pre-enrichment) — this has been set since Session 45 and shouldn't need re-doing, but if a fresh import's enrichment run fails with a 401, check this first before assuming a code bug.

## Workflows

- enrich-metadata.yml: daily 07:00 UTC; Google Books + Open Library →
  data/enrichedMetadata.json. Also usable for tag audits (publisher categories).
- tag-books.yml: manual; Claude Haiku 4.5 tags from descriptions; needs
  ANTHROPIC_API_KEY repo secret (unused so far — Session 12 tagged by hand).
- enrich-covers.yml: Sundays 05:00 UTC; backfills cover URLs (OpenLibrary +
  Google Books) via enrich_covers.py.
- enrich-isbn.yml: Sundays 04:00 UTC; backfills ISBN13 via enrich_isbn.py.
- enrich-goodreads.yml: manual; last-resort description scrape of each book's
  own Goodreads page via enrich_from_goodreads.py.
- scrape-ratings.yml: 5x/day; Amazon + StoryGraph ratings via Playwright
  (scrape_ratings.py), batched to avoid rate limits.
- sync-goodreads.yml: twice daily (6 AM / 6 PM UTC); syncs read/to-read
  shelves from Goodreads RSS via sync_goodreads.py.
- sync-currently-reading.yml: 4x/day; syncs the currently-reading shelf via
  sync_currently_reading.py into data/currentlyReading.json.
- export-extract.yml: Mondays 08:00 UTC; runs scripts/export_extract.js and
  commits a fresh dated `output/all-books-YYYY-MM-DD.csv` snapshot (previous
  dated files are kept, not overwritten). Requires this workflow file to live
  on `main` — GitHub only fires `schedule:` triggers for the default branch.
- data-quality-report.yml: daily 09:00 UTC (changed from weekly in Session
  13j); runs scripts/data_quality_report.js and commits a dated
  `output/data-quality-report-YYYY-MM-DD.md` + `.json` snapshot. Same
  main-branch requirement as export-extract.yml.
- audit-theme-tone.yml (Session 16c): hourly until every book is audited;
  runs audit_theme_tone.py (Claude + web search, real outside research per
  book — not just re-checking the cached enrichedMetadata.json description
  the tagging classifier already used) then scripts/apply_audit_corrections.mjs
  (auto-applies only high-confidence corrections; anything less goes to
  output/tone-theme-audit-log.jsonl for review). Needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
  (already a real repo secret per tag-books.yml). Real, ongoing API cost
  (billed web-search tool) — consider disabling once data/toneThemeAuditState.json
  shows the backlog cleared. Same main-branch requirement as the others.
- trakt-enrich-tmdb.yml (Session 44): daily 08:00 UTC; runs trakt/enrich_tmdb.py,
  fetching genres/cast/crew/keywords/similar-titles from TMDB for any title
  in trakt/data/watchlist.json or library.json not yet cached in
  trakt/data/enrichedMetadata.json. Needs a TMDB_API_KEY repo secret Bill
  must create (not yet set as of Session 44 — until it exists, BMTRE's
  recommend.html runs correctly but shows "not enough data yet" for every
  title). Same resumable-via-cache-membership pattern as enrich-metadata.yml.
- trakt-enrich-omdb.yml (Session 49): daily 09:00 UTC (after the TMDB job);
  runs trakt/enrich_omdb.py, fetching Rotten Tomatoes/Metacritic scores and
  awards data from OMDb for any title with a known IMDb id not yet cached in
  trakt/data/omdbMetadata.json. Needs an OMDB_API_KEY repo secret Bill must
  create (not yet set as of Session 49 — until it exists, the dashboard's
  Audience Score/Awards columns render blank, not an error).
- Data conflicts: sync + enrichment commit daily. Rebase carefully; prefer
  re-layering enrichment fields (themes/tones/similarToTitles) onto upstream.

## Bill's Taste Rules (explicit, from Session 12 review)

- NO fiction set before 1900. Nonfiction pre-1900 settings are sometimes fine.
  Exception: Lonesome Dove (kept for its reputation). When tagging or
  dismissing, check historical fiction settings against this rule.
- Recommend book 1 of a series, not mid-series entries, unless Bill has read
  the earlier books (engine: unread-series #2+ penalty in seriesSignal).
  Exception: Th1rt3en (#4) explicitly kept in Session 12 review.

## Data Caveats

- Session 12 similarToTitles are validated-to-exist but unverified-as-good:
  picked from model knowledge, not measured. Treat as draft; description
  similarity can eventually audit them.
- All 136 DNFs carry myRating=2 as a placeholder. Both engines now use
  reason-scaled virtual ratings (resolved Session 12b; circumstantial DNFs
  are skipped in the author signal).
- similarToTitles entries MUST be exact 5★ read titles (norm() tolerates
  series suffixes, but stay exact).

(3) Non-canonical themes — promoted `legal`/`courtroom` to canonical vocabulary (142 of 156 uses; already load-bearing in app.js's THEME_MACROS "Legal" filter chip, so they were functioning, just undocumented) and hand-remapped the remaining 14: "historical fiction"→"historical" (6), "coming-of-age" dropped (4, each book already had 2-3 other canonical themes), "fantasy"→"speculative" or dropped depending on whether the book already had it (2), "science"→"history" (1, judgment call — no exact nonfiction-science canonical bucket exists), "medical thriller" dropped (1). Also corrected a factual error in the report's own copy: "non-canonical themes don't contribute to themeBonus scoring" was false — engine.js's `fiveStarThemes` map has no canonical filter at all, so non-canonical themes score identically; the real cost is vocabulary fragmentation, not lost signal. (4) Broken similarToTitles refs touching a 5★ read — investigating the reported 61 surfaced a second **script bug**: `findNearMatch()`'s substring check (min length 5) and flat Levenshtein≤3 threshold produced confident-looking but wrong matches on short titles ("Grant" is a substring of "flagrant"; "It"/"We"/"Joe" are Levenshtein-distance-2 from "Me"; auditing all 28 pre-fix Levenshtein hits found exactly 1 real catch and 27 false positives) — raised the substring minimum to 8 chars and gated Levenshtein to titles ≥15 chars with a distance ratio ≤10% instead of a flat ≤3, which correctly reclassified 24 of the reported 61 as orphaned (real books just not in the dataset) and left 35 genuine subtitle-truncation citations (e.g. "The Big Short" → "The Big Short: Inside the Doomsday Machine", "Going Infinite", "Dream Team", "The Everything Store", "Fantasyland", "The Extra 2%", "The Innovators", etc.) which were corrected to their exact dataset title via a line-verified splice script (each edit asserted the expected old string was present before writing, so a stale line number fails loud instead of silently corrupting a neighboring line). Result: brokenNearMissTouchingFiveStar 61→0. **Recalibration**: reworked `computeQualityScore` — original rubric divided raw issue counts by huge denominators (total citations/theme tags across 1118 books), mathematically crushing any real count into a fraction of a point, and excluded whole categories (orphaned refs, duplicate-book groups) from scoring entirely. New version uses smaller, more honest denominators (5★-read count instead of total-citation count for the foundational check; total book count for duplicates), folds orphaned refs and duplicate-book groups into Hygiene/Safety at reduced weight, and raises penalty multipliers substantially (e.g. already-read-in-pool rate multiplier 3→8, self-referential 2→4, non-canonical 2→4); component weights shifted from a flat-ish 30/30/20/20 to 15/30/25/30 (Critical Field Health de-emphasized since it was already near-100% and had no real room to be critical; weight redistributed to Foundational/Safety/Hygiene where the real findings live). Tuned by hand against the exact pre-fix Jul 30 snapshot (5 already-read-in-pool, 19 self-referential, 61 broken-touching-5★, 156 non-canonical, 418 orphaned, 12 duplicate groups) to land at 65/100 — an intentional recalibration of the bar for "Excellent," not a re-derivation of the old thresholds. After the fixes above landed, the live score is **93/100** (up from the old rubric's 91, but now honestly earned rather than diluted); remaining impediments are 463 orphaned refs (documented as mostly legitimate, still scored at reduced weight), 52 candidate-to-candidate near-miss refs, and 5 duplicate-book groups (to-read books also sitting in a candidate pool — distinct from the already-read bug, lower severity, not yet fixed). | 16 (Jul 31 2026, claude code) | Redesigned the tone vocabulary from scratch per Bill's explicit request ("a set of 24 tones... no tone should account for more than 15% of books... do deep research and put together a plan") after establishing that the dataset actually had 39 different free-form tags in use, with `accessible` (38%), `narrative-driven` (37%), and `fast-paced` (31%) each diluting the one real tone-based signal (`bbreEngine.js`'s `buildToneProfile()`/`toneSignal()`) past the point of usefulness. Planned via EnterPlanMode/ExitPlanMode given the scale (1,111 books, 4 codebase touch-points) — see plan file for full research notes. **New 24-tone vocabulary** (documented above in its own section): 7 pacing/structure, 6 mood, 3 voice/humor, 4 craft/style, 4 intellectual-register tones. **Migration**: hand-tagging 1,111 books wasn't practical, and an LLM pass was both untested and something Bill had already opted out of, so built a deterministic scored classifier instead — scored every book against all 24 tones using its real `enrichedMetadata.json` description text (100% coverage confirmed), themes (inverse-frequency weighted so mega-themes like `narrative nonfiction` at 481 books don't dominate), and its *existing* legacy tone tags as a strong prior (carries forward prior human judgment rather than discarding it). Iterated the scoring three times after catching real problems: (1) naive keyword lists matched generic blurb hype-language ("forensic" substring-matched "forensics" in a non-procedural cadaver-science book; "social commentary" theme wrongly implied `satirical` on an analytical NBA book) — tightened to more specific phrases. (2) A flat "any positive score” rule let weak theme-only signals become default filler on hundreds of books (`lyrical`/`inspiring`/`thoughtful` matched 100-142 books off theme alone with zero keyword or prior support) — added a `MIN_SCORE=1.0` floor so a tag needs a real keyword hit or legacy-prior match, with theme affinity alone capped low enough to only break ties. (3) The cap-enforcement rebalancing loop initially reassigned an over-cap book's tone to *whatever tone had spare capacity*, regardless of fit — caught via spot-check ("The Good Girl," a Mary Kubica psychological thriller, lost both its real `dark`/`tense` tags to array-order ties among 254 equally-scored books and got assigned `nonlinear`/`ensemble` instead) — root cause was arbitrary tie-breaking among identically-scored legacy-only carryovers; fixed by rewriting as a single global greedy pass (sort all (book,tone) pairs by score desc, breaking ties by scarcity-of-alternatives so a book with only 2 viable tones is processed before one with 10) plus a capped-weight `weakThemes` layer so the 2-tone floor backfill has *some* real ranking signal instead of defaulting to a zero-score coin-flip. Also discovered en route: 13 books (the ones hand-tagged in Session 15) carried a **duplicate `"tones"` key** — Session 15's insertion script appended a new tag line instead of replacing the pre-existing empty `"tones": []`, so JSON.parse silently took the last key (the engine read the right value all along) but the file itself had 13 dead/shadowed lines; this migration's apply script detects and removes them as part of the same surgical pass. **Result**: all 24 tones ≤166 books (14.9%, the exact cap), graded tail down to `lyrical` (3 books) — a legitimately rare craft-quality descriptor no blurb reliably signals, same as the intentionally-rare `ensemble` (11) and `nonlinear` (16) structural tags. Applied via the same surgical text-splice technique as Sessions 13b/13c/15 (never JSON.parse+stringify a whole file) — verified every book's final tones exactly match the migration plan and zero non-tones fields changed, via a full round-trip diff, not just a sample. Also fixed a subtle ordering issue while validating: `bbreEngine.js`'s diversity re-ranking keys off `primaryTone(book)` = `tones[0]`, but the greedy assignment pass built each book's array in *global* cross-book processing order, which has nothing to do with that book's own confidence ranking — added a final per-book sort (each book's own tones by its own score) so `tones[0]` is always its most-confident match, the same property a human tagger's array order would have had. **Codebase updated in sync**: `bbreEngine.js`'s `THEME_TONES_MAP` (theme→tone fallback) and `TONE_PRIORITY` (tie-break order) rewritten to the new 24 words — also fixed a latent dead key (`historical fiction` never matched any real theme, which is `historical`); `tag_with_haiku.py`'s `TONES` constant and `app.js`'s `TONE_FILTER_ORDER` (live site tone filter chips) updated too. **NEW**: `findNonCanonicalTones()` in `scripts/data_quality_report.js`, mirroring the theme vocabulary's existing check (0 violations right after this migration, as expected) — deliberately *not* wired into `computeQualityScore()`, since folding it in would silently move the calibrated 65-baseline score without Bill asking for a fresh recalibration, the exact drift Session 14b pushed back on; reported only, plus a new dashboard tab. **Verification found a real bug and a real trade-off**: `scripts/validate_review.js` initially flagged 3 new failures beyond the single pre-existing Heartland one (The Tenant, The Wise Men, Empire of AI, all previously top-25). Root-caused before asking Bill anything: (1) a genuine classifier bug — the legacy-tag mapping sent `polemic` → `satirical` (the single worst-rated tone in Bill's whole profile, -0.375★), wrongly tagging 3 serious AI/tech-industry nonfiction books (Empire of AI, The Age of AI and Our Human Future, Nexus) as satirical; fixed the mapping to `polemic` → `thoughtful`/`investigative` and corrected all 3 books directly. (2) The remaining 3 failures traced to `bbreEngine.js`'s author-diversity MMR (penalty up to 0.30, steep) — since every book's tones changed at once, tiny toneSignal shifts reshuffled which specific title from a prolific author (e.g. Freida McFadden, many books in the pool) wins the author's top slot in the greedy re-ranking; a book itself didn't get worse, a sibling edged past it by a hair and the steep per-author penalty did the rest. Not fixable without either reverting the redesign or hand-rigging specific books' tags against their real classifier signal, so surfaced to Bill directly (via AskUserQuestion) rather than silently shipped or endlessly chased — his call: **proceed as-is**, these are ranks 28/47/76 of 1,100+ candidates, not banished, and forcing exact rank preservation would undermine the point of the redesign. `scripts/eval.js` (the precision@k gate) is structurally unaffected regardless — confirmed via grep that `rateEngine.js`, which the eval gate actually uses, doesn't read tones at all — and unchanged in practice (p10=100, p25=96, MAE=0.769). Data Quality Score unchanged at 67/100 (tones population was already ~100%, this was a value redistribution, not a completeness change). One important process note for future sessions: **do not re-run a migration script against already-migrated data** — mid-session, a second run of the classifier was accidentally pointed at the just-written new-tone files instead of the original legacy tags, silently corrupting the signal (caught by a distribution sanity check, not by luck); recovered via `git checkout` on the still-uncommitted data files and rebuilding cleanly from the original legacy tags. |
| 16b (Jul 31 2026, claude code) | Bill asked to re-verify the theme/tone distinction, twice — first a quick check, then "do a thorough recheck." **Quick pass**: confirmed zero string overlap between the 37 canonical themes and 24 canonical tones, and zero books carrying the same value in both fields; added permanent guardrails rather than a one-time check — a load-time assertion in `data_quality_report.js` that throws if the two vocabularies ever collide, plus a new `findThemeToneCollisions()` per-book integrity check (reported in the .md report and a new dashboard note). **Thorough pass found a real, more subtle problem**: cross-tabulating themes against tones showed roughly half the canonical themes — every one with under ~90 occurrences (legal, courtroom, domestic suspense, romance, horror, noir, food, psychology, political, military, and more) — correlated 85-100% with a specific tone (legal→procedural 100%, domestic suspense→compulsive/twisty 100%/98%, romance→heartwarming 100%, food→conversational 100%). Root cause: the classifier's inverse-frequency theme-weight formula (`min(1.5, 60/n*1.5)`) was designed to give rarer themes *more* credit per occurrence, but for any theme with ≤90 uses that credit alone exceeded `MIN_SCORE` (1.0) — meaning tone assignment for roughly half the dataset's theme-taggings was fully determined by theme membership alone, with no chance for the specific book's actual description or prior tagging to differentiate it. Tones were re-encoding themes for these books, not adding independent signal — the opposite of the distinction Bill asked to verify. Fixed in two steps: (1) capped the per-theme weight formula at 0.8 (below `MIN_SCORE`) instead of 1.5; (2) found the cap alone was insufficient — 55 of 86 legal-themed books also carry `courtroom`, both mapped to `procedural`, so two 0.8 credits could still stack past 1.0 — fixed by capping the *total* theme-affinity contribution per tone at 0.8 (via `Math.max` across a tone's matching themes, not `+=` per match), not per individual theme. Re-ran the classifier against the original pre-migration legacy tags (careful this time to source from `git show` on the pre-migration commit, not the working tree, per 16's process note) and re-verified the correlation matrix: legal→procedural dropped 100%→82.6%, domestic suspense→compulsive 100%→31.2%, romance→heartwarming 100%→87.5%, contemporary→character-driven 64%→6.2% (later confirmed as a legitimate correction, not over-shrinkage — checked the 15 legal-themed books that lost `procedural` and each got a more specific, better-fitting alternative instead, e.g. "The Genome Defense" → `investigative`/`revelatory`). A few pairs remain genuinely high (noir→dark 100%, food→conversational 100%, humor→funny 93%) — checked these against the "theme-only" diagnostic and confirmed they're backed almost entirely by real keyword/legacy-prior evidence, not theme-shortcut (0-1 theme-only assignments each), so the high correlation reflects a real, evidence-based pattern rather than an artifact — these theme/tone pairs are close to definitionally linked in actual usage (a "noir" book being "dark" isn't tone re-encoding theme, it's just true). Re-verified quality with a full 24-tone sample audit (~70 books) after the fix — no new bad assignments found. **Re-applied and re-verified**: `scripts/eval.js` unchanged (p10=100, p25=96, MAE=0.769, still unaffected by design). `scripts/validate_review.js` shows one additional near-miss beyond the three from Session 16 (The Trolls of Wall Street, rank 26 — one over the cutoff) from the same author-diversity-MMR reshuffling mechanism Bill already reviewed and approved proceeding with in 16; not a new category of problem, just one more book affected by a second round of dataset-wide tone changes. Data Quality Score unchanged at 67/100; `findNonCanonicalTones()` and `findThemeToneCollisions()` both report zero. |

Marks each applied correction with an append-only follow-up log line (`applied: true`) rather than mutating the original line, so the log stays a durable, append-only audit trail even for auto-applied fixes. Confirmed via Bill's explicit choice (asked first, given ~2+ days of unattended runs and real billed API cost): auto-fix only high-confidence findings; medium/low/insufficient-evidence ones stay in the log for a human or future session to review, never touching the data. New workflow `.github/workflows/audit-theme-tone.yml` (hourly `schedule` + `workflow_dispatch` with a configurable batch size, default 20/hour → ~56 hours to clear the full backlog), modeled directly on `enrich-metadata.yml`'s shape. Tested both scripts end-to-end before relying on the real workflow: `audit_theme_tone.py` dry-run with a mocked API response confirmed batching, resumability (a second run correctly skipped already-audited books), and log/state file writing; `apply_audit_corrections.mjs` dry-run against a copied data set with 3 fabricated log entries (one real high-confidence fix, one medium-confidence tone suggestion, one bad-vocabulary theme suggestion) confirmed only the genuine high-confidence "Tumor" fix was applied, the other two were correctly left untouched, the diff was a single exact line, JSON stayed valid, and a second run was a correct no-op (idempotent). | 16e (Jul 31 2026, claude code) | Got the real `audit-theme-tone.yml` workflow working end-to-end after 3 failed scheduled runs. Root causes, in the order hit: (1) `git add data/ output/tone-theme-audit-log.jsonl` in one command failed hard (`fatal: pathspec ... did not match any files`, exit 128) on the very first run, since the log file doesn't exist until the first successful audit — split into `git add data/` then a separate `if [ -f output/tone-theme-audit-log.jsonl ]; then git add ...; fi` (an `[ -f X ] && cmd` one-liner would have had the same bug under `bash -e`, which aborts the step when the test is false, not just when `cmd` fails — used the `if` form deliberately). (2) All 3 scheduled runs were also silently doing zero real work: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was empty/401 in the job logs — a user-side GitHub secret misconfiguration, not fixable by me directly; walked Bill through getting a real key from console.anthropic.com and setting it as the exact-named repo secret. Along the way found and fixed a masking bug: `audit_theme_tone.py` catches per-book exceptions by design (one bad book shouldn't kill a whole batch), but that meant a batch where literally every book failed still reported the job as a green "success" — added a check that `sys.exit()`s loudly when `failures == len(batch)`. (3) First real run with the fixed key still failed every book with JSON parse errors ("Unterminated string", "Expecting value") — root cause: the server-side `web_search` tool's results count against the same `max_tokens` output budget as the final JSON answer, so `max_tokens=1500` was silently truncating mid-string on every book once real search results were involved. Raised to 4096, added an explicit `stop_reason == "max_tokens"` check (fails with a clear message instead of a confusing downstream parse error), and made parse failures include a tail snippet of the raw response for future diagnosability. Tested both the normal-response and simulated-truncated-response paths with mocked `call_claude` before pushing. **Verified real end-to-end success** (run `30634167512`, batch_size 3, commit `4f76196`): 3 books genuinely researched with real citations and sources (The Note, and Joe Dillard books 1-2 by Scott Pratt) — one medium-confidence tone finding correctly held for review, not auto-applied (In Good Faith's `procedural` tag looks weaker than book 1's per a StoryGraph review calling out the missing procedural elements — suggested `dark`/`gritty` instead); one low-confidence theme finding correctly downgraded by the vocabulary guardrail (The Note's suggested replacement included the non-canonical "psychological suspense"); `themeToneReviewed` flipped true on all 3; state file and log committed and pushed clean to `main`. The hourly schedule is now live and unattended on `main` with a working pipeline — next check-in should confirm the backlog is actually shrinking (1,108 of 1,111 remaining as of this run) and periodically skim the log's medium/low-confidence queue for human review. |

Splitting these four into smaller buckets wouldn't fix a signal-collapse problem (there isn't one for themes), and inventing sub-buckets that don't reflect genuine reader-preference clusters is the exact failure mode Session 16b already hit and rejected for tones. **Recommendation, approved by Bill via plan mode**: leave `thriller`/`history`/`memoir`/`speculative` alone (documented here so a future session doesn't re-open this without re-deriving the analysis); `literary` stays a live option pending a separate decision, not pursued this session. **One real bug found and fixed during the scoping pass**: `scripts/apply_audit_corrections.mjs` keeps its own separate `CANON_THEMES` copy (distinct from the other six vocabulary copies), and it still contained `narrative nonfiction` — missed during the Session 19 retirement. His loved shows also happen to cluster tightly in one dense crime-drama network (Power franchise, Mayans M.C./Sons of Anarchy universe, Snowfall, BMF — all heavily cross-cited by TMDB's own recommendations algorithm), so that signal was maxing out for shows (Mayans M.C.: 5 forward + 10 reverse matches, both capped) far more than for movies (most movies: exactly 1 forward match, `Southpaw`/`Lone Survivor`/`The Banker` etc. all landing at the identical 62.0). **Found one real, separate bug along the way**: TMDB uses two different genre vocabularies — movies get "Action"/"Science Fiction"/"War", shows get "Action & Adventure"/"Sci-Fi & Fantasy"/"War & Politics"/"Kids" for the same concepts — left unnormalized, a loved movie's "Action" (15 uses) and a loved show's "Action & Adventure" (7 uses) were accumulating into separate `lovedGenres` buckets instead of a combined 22, silently diluting genre-preference credit. Fixed via `GENRE_ALIASES`/`normalizeGenre()`, applied at both index-build and lookup time — real fix, but verified via the same diagnostic that it wasn't the actual driver of the score gap (most top candidates already hit `genreBonus`'s own +8 cap either way, pre- and post-fix). Verified: Gladiator (real Ridley Scott director match + 2 forward + 1 reverse match) now scores 99.8/100 instead of capping at 85.9; movies scoring ≥63 went from 1 of 116 to 11; confirmed live on the dashboard (Gladiator renders 100, three more movies now in the 70s). What causes it?")**: built a live score-component diagnostic (reusable at `/tmp/.../diagnose_movie_scores.mjs`, not committed) and found the real cause with numbers, not a guess — no movie candidate combined the two largest scoring signals (a loved-director match, worth up to +15, and a forward/reverse similar-title match, worth up to ~28 combined). The candidates with a title-similarity match (Southpaw, Big George Foreman, Lone Survivor) had zero director overlap; the candidates with a real director match (Kingdom of Heaven/Ridley Scott, Only the Brave/Kosinski, The Hangover/Todd Phillips, Inside Llewyn Davis/Joel Coen) had zero similar-title overlap. Not a scoring bug — `matchPointScale()` already compensates correctly for the raw loved-movie-vs-loved-show pool-size gap on the *scoring* side. Root cause was one level upstream: `discover_candidates.js` ranked movies and shows together in one combined top-150-by-citation-count list, and since Bill has ~2x as many loved shows as loved movies, shows structurally dominated — the real pool had 106 movies vs. 221 shows despite 1,575 untapped movie candidates sitting in the citation reservoir. **Fixed**: `discover_candidates.js` now ranks and caps per type instead of globally (same type-imbalance-compensation principle as `matchPointScale`, one step earlier in the pipeline — a movie can't compete in scoring if it never reaches the candidate pool at all). Re-ran it: added 150 more movies (106→256) and 150 more shows, pure local computation from already-cached TMDB citation data, no network calls. **Then Bill asked to cap the pool at 100 movies + 100 shows, evicting low scorers as high scorers are added.** New `trakt/scripts/prune_candidate_pool.js`: scores every candidate with the exact same `matchScore()`/`buildIndexes()` the dashboard uses (so eviction risk always matches what actually renders), keeps the top 100 per type, evicts the rest; also sweeps out anything gone stale (watched/watchlisted since being added) as a bonus hygiene pass. Critically, a candidate with no `enrichedMetadata.json` entry yet is *never* evicted regardless of the cap — a freshly-discovered stub would score near-zero on missing data alone, and evicting it unscored would be the opposite of "kick out the ones that score low" (those need a fair chance first). **Then Bill asked for a weekly job to keep pulling new candidates.** New `.github/workflows/trakt-discover-candidates.yml` (Sundays 10 AM UTC, after both daily enrichment jobs, plus `workflow_dispatch` with tunable inputs): runs discover → TMDB-enrich → prune in sequence each week. Building this surfaced a real interaction bug between the two new features before it ever shipped: pruning *deletes* an evicted candidate from `candidatePool.json`, the only place the old "already known" check looked — so on a recurring schedule, a title evicted one week for scoring low would look brand-new again the next week, get rediscovered, and likely get re-evicted in a permanent loop. Fixed with a new `trakt/data/discoveredHistory.json` (append-only, seeded with all 627 titleKeys already in the pool at the time) that `discover_candidates.js` now checks first — a title is only ever evaluated once, no matter how many times it falls out of the active pool. Prune step has `if: always()` so a bad/expired TMDB key (a real failure mode, proven the same night) doesn't also block pruning the already-enriched backlog. **Then TMDB itself started failing** with a bare 401 partway through this stretch, after having worked all session (a real successful run at 6:36 PM) — Bill confirmed he hadn't touched the secret, ruling out an accidental-overwrite explanation, and no TMDB-wide outage was found either. Rather than keep asking Bill to investigate a secret he couldn't diagnose from the GitHub UI, improved `enrich_tmdb.py`'s `get_json()` to capture and surface TMDB's actual error-response body (not just the bare status code) on the next run — revealed the definitive answer directly from TMDB itself: `{"status_code":7,"status_message":"Invalid API key: You must be granted a valid key.","success":false}`. A genuinely dead credential per TMDB's own system, not a code bug, not an outage, not a rate limit — confirmed rather than guessed. Bill generated a fresh key and updated the secret; verified immediately with a real 300-title batch (run `32526896678`, succeeded). Pulled the enrichment commit, re-ran `prune_candidate_pool.js` now that all 300 new candidates had real scores to compete on — **pool is now cleanly 100 movies / 100 shows**. Name it with the date."** New `trakt/scripts/lib/loadAllTitles.js` — a shared full-row loader mirroring `scripts/lib/loadData.js`'s role for the book project (one join, so a future consumer can't silently drift into mismatched logic) — merges `library.json`/`watchlist.json`/`candidatePool.json` into one row set tagged by status (Watched/Watchlist/Candidate), joined against TMDB metadata, OMDb metadata, feedback/exclusion state, and the engine's own live-computed scores. 56 columns, not a curated UI subset. New `trakt/scripts/export_extract.js` writes `trakt/output/all-titles-YYYY-MM-DD.csv` (dated, one file per run, same convention as the book side's `output/all-books-*.csv`). Missing OMDb data contributes 0 either way, never a penalty. `matchScore()`/`reason()`/`rankRecommendations()`/`rankAll()` all gained an `omdbMeta` parameter (default `{}`, backward compatible) threaded through to every real caller (`dashboard.js`'s rec panels + All Titles table, `recommend.js` — which never even loaded `omdbMetadata.json` before this, `prune_candidate_pool.js`, `loadAllTitles.js`) so the score shown anywhere in the app now matches what actually drives ranking; `reason()` gained two new explanation tiers (well-reviewed / real award recognition) for candidates whose best evidence is critical reception rather than a director/similar-title/genre match. **Verified with a live diagnostic comparing scores with vs. without the new signal** (not just that it runs): 89 of 227 live candidates moved, deltas bounded exactly as designed (max +10, max -6), zero candidates hit the 100 clamp either way — real, sane movement.
